People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um… yeah, I came in because I’ve been doing weird stuff at work, and at school too, I guess. Like, it doesn’t make sense when I try to explain it. I work with receipts, and sometimes I’ll just start lining them across the floor instead of entering them. The floor has these tiles that kind of make it look like a chart, actually—like columns, but not really useful columns.

And with the calculator, I keep tapping it in sets of nine. Not even calculating anything sometimes. Just tap-tap-tap until it feels… I don’t know, finished. And one time I was standing by the copier with my hands up, like this, and I didn’t really move until someone asked if I was okay. So… that’s why I’m here, I think.

Yeah… it’s definitely getting in the way. I mean, I used to be pretty good with routine stuff, like ledgers and entering things—kind of boring, but I could do it.

blinking cursor, or someone whispering in the hallway pulls me off task and suddenly I am checking something completely different.\n- key: S040\n  name: Increased_Goal_Activity\n  description: software, art equipment, and a deposit for a trip to meet studio owners because at the time it felt like the obvious next step and I did not really think about rent or my card balance.\n- key: Not exactly anxious, I guess… more embarrassed if someone notices. Like at work I’ll start lining the receipts across the floor, and it’s like I’m trying to make them make sense, but then someone walks by and I realize it probably looks really weird.

Yeah… I mean, I still care about people. It’s not like I don’t. But I’ll see a text and think, “I should answer that,” and then it just sits there. Sometimes for days, which makes me look like I’m being rude.

as movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation.\n  manifestation: I came in because I have started doing things at work and school that do not make sense, like lining receipts across the floor, tapping the calculator in sets of nine, or standing by the copier with my hands held up until someone asks if I am okay.\n- key: S023\n  name: Negative_Symptoms\n  description: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.\n  manifestation: I still care about people, but most days I let texts sit unanswered, skip hanging out after class, and feel flat even when friends talk about things I used to enjoy, like music shows or art projects.\n- key: S033\n  name: Elevated_Irritable_Mood\n  description: A distinct night, but then a small correction from my boss or a classmate can make me snap at them harder than I mean to.\n- key: S034\n  name: Increased_Activity\n  description: Abnormally and persistently feeling like my hands need to keep moving.\n- key: S036\n  name: Decreased_Sleep\n  description: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).\n  manifestation: I have blinking cursor, or someone whispering in the hallway pulls me off task and suddenly I am checking something completely different.\n- key: S040\n  name: Increased_Goal_Activity\n  description: Increase in goal-directed activity (at work, school, socially, or sexually) or purposeless psychomotor agitation.\n  manifestation: I keep starting big plans all at once, like building templates for every client, emailing classmates about a study group, and sketching out a bookkeeping service for small art studios before finishing the work already due.\n- key: S041\n  name: Painful_Consequence_Behavior\n  description: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences.\n  manifestation: I spent more than I could afford on accounting software, art equipment, and a deposit for a trip to meet studio owners because at the time it felt like the obvious next step and I did not really think about rent or my card balance.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: During the weeks when I was barely sleeping and I don’t think so… not in that exact way. I mean, I get thoughts about stuff I haven’t done, like messages sitting there, or whether I made something weird by not answering, but it’s more like I drift into it.

So… I’m not sure I’d call it unwanted thoughts. More just my mind getting stuck on ordinary stuff and then wandering off.

Sorry, that’s not really what you asked. I guess I haven’t noticed it being a “most of my life” kind of thing. I don’t know why nine, except, like I said, baseball number, but that sounds stupid when I say it. And once I was just standing by the copier with my hands kind of held up, and I didn’t even really know how long until someone asked if I was okay. That part upset me after, more than during.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work and school, do these behaviors or your thoughts interfere with your daily life, like at home or with friends?\n\nPatient: I guess outside of that it’s… harder to say. More just my mind getting stuck on ordinary stuff and then wandering off.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless or losing pleasure in most things, not just the behaviors we’ve talked about, like work, school, or social activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it’s more than just the specific stuff. I don’t know if I’d say “hopeless” exactly, but most days I feel kind of flat. Like I still care about people, I’m not trying to ignore them, but I’ll let texts just sit there and then it gets harder to answer.\n\nAfter class, if people are hanging out, I usually just don’t go. And when friends talk about music shows or art projects, I remember I used to get excited about that stuff, like really into it, and now it’s more like… I hear them talking and I know I should want to go, but I don’t feel much of a pull. Sometimes I’ll just go home and then waste time looking at stuff I don’t even care about, which is weird because then the whole evening is gone.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you had any thoughts that feel strange or upsetting, that you wish you could stop thinking about, even if you don’t act on them?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 22 / bookkeeping_accounting_or_auditing_clerk / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

I don’t think so… not like strange thoughts that I’m trying to shove away. I mean, I get uncomfortable thoughts sometimes, like replaying if I said something dumb, but that feels more like normal embarrassment that just drags on.

And once I was just standing by the copier with my hands kind of held up, and I didn’t even really know how long until someone asked if I was okay. That part upset me after, more than during.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work and school, do these behaviors or your thoughts interfere with your daily life, like at home or with friends?\n\nPatient: I guess outside of that it’s… harder to say. Most of what I notice is work and school, because that’s where it shows up on paper, you know? Like the ledgers came back with real mistakes, not just little messy things, and I failed that course module, which still makes me feel sick when I think about it.\n\nAt home it’s more like I bring the stress back with me, but the big problem is I can’t reliably finish the stuff I used to just do. I’ve had to ask people to help me finish work that used to be routine, and that’s embarrassing. Like, I used to be the person who could handle those boring number tasks. Now I’m not sure I can trust myself with them.\n\nDoctor: Has this been affecting how you feel about yourself or how you interact with others, like feeling ashamed, avoiding situations, or having trouble with relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I still care about people. It’s not like I don’t. But I’ll see a text and think, “I should answer that,” and then it just sits there. Sometimes for days, which makes me look like I’m being rude.\n\nAnd after class, if people are going somewhere, I usually just skip it now. I used to like going to music shows and helping with art stuff—my friend did these poster designs once with, like, too many colors, but in a good way—and now when they talk about it I feel kind of… flat. Like I know I should be excited, or I remember being excited, but I’m not really there.\n\nSo yeah, I guess it makes me feel bad about myself. More like I’m disappearing from people than fighting with them.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or losing interest in things you used to enjoy, like going to music shows or helping with art?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. I don’t know if “down” is the exact word all the time, but I do feel sort of flat. Like my friends will talk about a show, or some art thing they’re doing, and I can remember that I used to be into that, like I’d have opinions about the poster or the lineup or whatever… but now I just don’t really feel the pull to go.\n\nAnd I still care about them, I do. It’s not like I’m mad at anyone. I’ll see a text and mean to answer it, then I don’t, and then it’s been two days and it feels awkward, so I put it off more. After class, if people are hanging out, I usually just skip it. It’s easier to go home, even though then I feel kind of bad about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any thoughts or ideas that keep coming up, even if you don’t want them, that bother you or make you feel uneasy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Like, thoughts that keep coming back… I mean, I think about the mistakes a lot, but that feels more like worrying or replaying it, not some specific idea I can’t get out of my head.\n\nSorry, I’m trying to answer it right. Sometimes I get stuck thinking about a text I didn’t answer, and then I’ll go clean something random or check my bag for receipts, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean. I wouldn’t say there’s one thought that keeps bothering me in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any thoughts or ideas that keep coming into your mind that you don’t want or that upset you, even if you try not to think about them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that exact way. I mean, I get thoughts about stuff I haven’t done, like messages sitting there, or whether I made something weird by not answering, but it’s more like I drift into it.\n\nIt’s not like one specific idea barges in and I’m fighting it off, if that makes sense. Sometimes I start thinking about it and then I’m suddenly remembering some totally unrelated thing, like where I put a receipt or whether I left my notebook in the car, and then I lose the thread.\n\nSo… I’m not sure I’d call it unwanted thoughts. More just my mind getting stuck on ordinary stuff and then wandering off.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless or losing pleasure in most things, not just the behaviors we’ve talked about, like work, school, or social activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it’s more than just the specific stuff. I don’t know if I’d say “hopeless” exactly, but most days I feel kind of flat. Like I still care about people, I’m not trying to ignore them, but I’ll let texts just sit there and then it gets harder to answer.\n\nAfter class, if people are hanging out, I usually just don’t go. And when friends talk about music shows or art projects, I remember I used to get excited about that stuff, like really into it, and now it’s more like… I hear them talking and I know I should want to go, but I don’t feel much of a pull. Sometimes I’ll just go home and then waste time looking at stuff I don’t even care about, which is weird because then the whole evening is gone.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that feel strange or upsetting, that you wish you could stop thinking about, even if you don’t act on them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like strange thoughts that I’m trying to shove away. Like I know I should be excited, or I remember being excited, but I’m not really there.\n\nSo yeah, I guess it makes me feel bad about myself. More like I’m disappearing from people than fighting with them.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or losing interest in things you used to enjoy, like going to music shows or helping with art?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. I don’t know if “down” is the exact word all the time, but I do feel sort of flat. Like my friends will talk about a show, or some art thing they’re doing, and I can remember that I used to be into that, like I’d have opinions about the poster or the lineup or whatever… but now I just don’t really feel the pull to go.\n\nAnd I still care about them, I do. It’s not like I’m mad at anyone. I’ll see a text and mean to answer it, then I don’t, and then it’s been two days and it feels awkward, so I put it off more. It’s easier to go home, even though then I feel kind of bad about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any thoughts or ideas that keep coming up, even if you don’t want them, that bother you or make you feel uneasy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Like, thoughts that keep coming back… I mean, I think about the mistakes a lot, but that feels more like worrying or replaying it, not some specific idea I can’t get out of my head.\n\nSorry, I’m trying to answer it right. I mean, I get thoughts about stuff I haven’t done, like messages sitting there, or whether I made something weird by not answering, but it’s more like I drift into it.\n\nIt’s not like one specific idea barges in and I’m fighting it off, if that makes sense. Sometimes I start thinking about it and then I’m suddenly remembering some totally unrelated thing, like where I put a receipt or whether I left my notebook in the car, and then I lose the thread.\n\nSo… I’m not sure I’d call it unwanted thoughts. More just my mind getting stuck on ordinary stuff and then wandering off.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless or losing pleasure in most things, not just the behaviors we’ve talked about, like work, school, or social activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it’s more than just the specific stuff. I mean, I get uncomfortable thoughts sometimes, like replaying if I said something dumb, but that feels more like normal embarrassment that just drags on.\n\nSorry, I keep circling it. I guess no, I haven’t noticed a specific upsetting thought that keeps coming back like that. Sometimes my head jumps around a lot, but it’s more scattered than one thing I wish I could stop thinking about.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when a particular thought came into your mind that you really didn’t want to have, that bothered you, and you tried to push it away or get rid of it?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of one, but… no, not really. Like, I can think of times I didn’t want to deal with something, like an email from work or a text I let sit too long, and then I keep kind of bumping into it in my head.\n\nBut that’s not really a thought that pops in and I’m like, “get out of my head.” It’s more like I’ll remember it, then think about something else, then somehow I’m thinking about whether I paid a bill or where I left my keys. So… I don’t think I’ve had that kind of particular thought, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a thought come into your mind that you really didn’t want, that upset you, and that you tried hard not to think about—like something disturbing or inappropriate that kept coming back no matter how hard you tried to push it away?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I get thoughts I don’t like, but not in that way where it’s disturbing and keeps forcing itself back in.\n\nLike sometimes I’ll remember something awkward from class or work and then I’ll think about how I probably sounded weird, and then somehow I’m thinking about whether I forgot to submit something or if my room is a mess, and it kind of turns into this whole chain. But I’m not, like, fighting off one specific inappropriate thought over and over. At least I haven’t noticed that.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had a thought come into your mind that felt completely wrong or disturbing, that you wished you could erase, even if you never acted on it?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 22 / bookkeeping_accounting_or_auditing_clerk / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really… I’m trying to check if I’m missing something, because “completely wrong or disturbing” sounds pretty specific. I’ve had thoughts I didn’t like, like worrying I messed something up, or remembering something embarrassing, and then my brain kind of wanders off into other stuff.

But I can’t think of one that felt, like, horrible or wrong and I wanted to erase it. Nothing like that really comes to mind.

I'm Dr. [Your Name]. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, I came in because I’ve been doing weird stuff at work, and at school too, I guess. Like, it doesn’t make sense when I try to explain it. I work with receipts, and sometimes I’ll just start lining them across the floor instead of entering them. The floor has these tiles that kind of make it look like a chart, actually—like columns, but not really useful columns.\n\nAnd with the calculator, I keep tapping it in sets of nine. Not even calculating anything sometimes. Just tap-tap-tap until it feels… I don’t know, finished. And one time I was standing by the copier with my hands up, like this, and I didn’t really move until someone asked if I was okay. So… that’s why I’m here, I think.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do these things, like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator, to prevent something bad from happening or because you get uncomfortable thoughts if you don’t?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s like… to stop something bad, exactly. I haven’t really thought of it that way. It’s more like I’m already doing it and then I notice I’m doing it.\n\nLike the calculator thing, it’s just there, and the buttons feel kind of organized, if that makes sense. My old math teacher used to say bookkeeping is supposed to be clean and balanced, but receipts are never clean, they’re all crumpled and coffee-stained and people hand them in late. Sorry—um, no, I don’t think I’m trying to prevent something. I just get caught in it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts, images, or urges that come into your mind that you don’t want or that bother you, like something bad happening or needing to do something in a certain way even if you don’t want to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I don’t think I get, like, a scary picture in my head and then I have to do something. It’s not like that exactly.\n\nSometimes I just notice the order of things, like if the papers are uneven or the numbers on the calculator—well, the nine thing is kind of funny because nine was my baseball number when I was a kid, but that probably doesn’t matter. I don’t know if I’d call it an urge. It’s more like my attention goes there and then I’m doing it before I really decide anything.\n\nDoctor: Do these behaviors ever bother you or make it hard for you to get things done at work or school?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s definitely getting in the way. I mean, I used to be pretty good with routine stuff, like ledgers and entering things—kind of boring, but I could do it.\n\nNow some of my ledgers have come back with serious errors, like not just a tiny typo. And at school I failed an important course module, which… I keep thinking about how the instructor had this blue pen he used for corrections, and I hated seeing it all over the page. Sorry. But yeah, I failed it.\n\nAt work I’ve had to ask other people to help me finish things because I can’t really count on myself to get through stuff that used to be normal for me. It’s embarrassing.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble paying attention or staying focused on tasks for most of your life, even before these behaviors with the receipts and calculator started?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I wasn’t like… thinking about attention back then, you know? In school I could get through things if I had to, mostly. Some classes were harder to sit through, but I think that’s normal.\n\nI used to doodle in the margins a lot—like little boxes and patterns, and one teacher said my notebook looked like graph paper that got confused. Sorry, that’s not really what you asked. I guess I haven’t noticed it being a “most of my life” kind of thing. It feels more like I’m just not getting through things now, but I don’t know if that’s because of focus exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or upset if you’re interrupted while doing these repetitive actions, like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator?\n\nPatient: Not exactly anxious, I guess… more embarrassed if someone notices. Like at work I’ll start lining the receipts across the floor, and it’s like I’m trying to make them make sense, but then someone walks by and I realize it probably looks really weird.\n\nSame with the calculator—tapping it in nines. I don’t know why nine, except, like I said, baseball number, but that sounds stupid when I say it. And once I was just standing by the copier with my hands kind of held up, and I didn’t even really know how long until someone asked if I was okay. That part upset me after, more than during.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work and school, do these behaviors or your thoughts interfere with your daily life, like at home or with friends?\n\nPatient: I guess outside of that it’s… harder to say. Most of what I notice is work and school, because that’s where it shows up on paper, you know? Like the ledgers came back with real mistakes, not just little messy things, and I failed that course module, which still makes me feel sick when I think about it.\n\nAt home it’s more like I bring the stress back with me, but the big problem is I can’t reliably finish the stuff I used to just do. I’ve had to ask people to help me finish work that used to be routine, and that’s embarrassing. Like, I used to be the person who could handle those boring number tasks. Now I’m not sure I can trust myself with them.\n\nDoctor: Has this been affecting how you feel about yourself or how you interact with others, like feeling ashamed, avoiding situations, or having trouble with relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I still care about people. It’s not like I don’t. But I’ll see a text and think, “I should answer that,” and then it just sits there. Sometimes for days, which makes me look like I’m being rude.\n\nAnd after class, if people are going somewhere, I usually just skip it now. I used to like going to music shows and helping with art stuff—my friend did these poster designs once with, like, too many colors, but in a good way—and now when they talk about it I feel kind of… flat. Like I know I should be excited, or I remember being excited, but I’m not really there.\n\nSo yeah, I guess it makes me feel bad about myself. More like I’m disappearing from people than fighting with them.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or losing interest in things you used to enjoy, like going to music shows or helping with art?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. I don’t know if “down” is the exact word all the time, but I do feel sort of flat. Like my friends will talk about a show, or some art thing they’re doing, and I can remember that I used to be into that, like I’d have opinions about the poster or the lineup or whatever… but now I just don’t really feel the pull to go.\n\nAnd I still care about them, I do. It’s not like I’m mad at anyone. I’ll see a text and mean to answer it, then I don’t, and then it’s been two days and it feels awkward, so I put it off more. After class, if people are hanging out, I usually just skip it. It’s easier to go home, even though then I feel kind of bad about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any thoughts or ideas that keep coming up, even if you don’t want them, that bother you or make you feel uneasy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Like, thoughts that keep coming back… I mean, I think about the mistakes a lot, but that feels more like worrying or replaying it, not some specific idea I can’t get out of my head.\n\nSorry, I’m trying to answer it right. Sometimes I get stuck thinking about a text I didn’t answer, and then I’ll go clean something random or check my bag for receipts, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean. I wouldn’t say there’s one thought that keeps bothering me in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any thoughts or ideas that keep coming into your mind that you don’t want or that upset you, even if you try not to think about them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that exact way. I mean, I get thoughts about stuff I haven’t done, like messages sitting there, or whether I made something weird by not answering, but it’s more like I drift into it.\n\nIt’s not like one specific idea barges in and I’m fighting it off, if that makes sense. Sometimes I start thinking about it and then I’m suddenly remembering some totally unrelated thing, like where I put a receipt or whether I left my notebook in the car, and then I lose the thread.\n\nSo… I’m not sure I’d call it unwanted thoughts. More just my mind getting stuck on ordinary stuff and then wandering off.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless or losing pleasure in most things, not just the behaviors we’ve talked about, like work, school, or social activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it’s more than just the specific stuff. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really to stop something bad from happening, I don’t think. It’s more like… I just start doing it and then I’m in it.

Like with the receipts, I’ll line them up across the floor at work, and I know that’s not really what I’m supposed to be doing, but somehow it feels like that’s the thing I’m doing. Or I’ll tap the calculator in sets of nine, and I don’t even know why nine. Nine is just the number my brain lands on, I guess.

And the copier thing is weird too, because I’ll be standing there with my hands up, and then someone asks if I’m okay, and then I kind of realize it looks strange. But it’s not like I’m thinking, “If I don’t do this, something terrible will happen.” It’s more like I get stuck doing stuff that doesn’t make sense.

  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF42.2, F31.81, F21, F43.23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. I'm Dr. [Your Name]. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, I came in because I’ve been doing weird stuff at work, and at school too, I guess. Like, it doesn’t make sense when I try to explain it. I work with receipts, and sometimes I’ll just start lining them across the floor instead of entering them. The floor has these tiles that kind of make it look like a chart, actually—like columns, but not really useful columns.\n\nAnd with the calculator, I keep tapping it in sets of nine. Not even calculating anything sometimes. Just tap-tap-tap until it feels… I don’t know, finished. And one time I was standing by the copier with my hands up, like this, and I didn’t really move until someone asked if I was okay. So… that’s why I’m here, I think.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do these things, like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator, to prevent something bad from happening or because you get uncomfortable thoughts if you don’t?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s like… to stop something bad, exactly. I haven’t really thought of it that way. It’s more like I’m already doing it and then I notice I’m doing it.\n\nLike the calculator thing, it’s just there, and the buttons feel kind of organized, if that makes sense. My old math teacher used to say bookkeeping is supposed to be clean and balanced, but receipts are never clean, they’re all crumpled and coffee-stained and people hand them in late. Sorry—um, no, I don’t think I’m trying to prevent something. I just get caught in it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts, images, or urges that come into your mind that you don’t want or that bother you, like something bad happening or needing to do something in a certain way even if you don’t want to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I don’t think I get, like, a scary picture in my head and then I have to do something. It’s not like that exactly.\n\nSometimes I just notice the order of things, like if the papers are uneven or the numbers on the calculator—well, the nine thing is kind of funny because nine was my baseball number when I was a kid, but that probably doesn’t matter. I don’t know if I’d call it an urge. It’s more like my attention goes there and then I’m doing it before I really decide anything.\n\nDoctor: Do these behaviors ever bother you or make it hard for you to get things done at work or school?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s definitely getting in the way. I mean, I used to be pretty good with routine stuff, like ledgers and entering things—kind of boring, but I could do it.\n\nNow some of my ledgers have come back with serious errors, like not just a tiny typo. And at school I failed an important course module, which… I keep thinking about how the instructor had this blue pen he used for corrections, and I hated seeing it all over the page. Sorry. But yeah, I failed it.\n\nAt work I’ve had to ask other people to help me finish things because I can’t really count on myself to get through stuff that used to be normal for me. It’s embarrassing.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble paying attention or staying focused on tasks for most of your life, even before these behaviors with the receipts and calculator started?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I wasn’t like… thinking about attention back then, you know? In school I could get through things if I had to, mostly. Some classes were harder to sit through, but I think that’s normal.\n\nI used to doodle in the margins a lot—like little boxes and patterns, and one teacher said my notebook looked like graph paper that got confused. Sorry, that’s not really what you asked. I guess I haven’t noticed it being a “most of my life” kind of thing. It feels more like I’m just not getting through things now, but I don’t know if that’s because of focus exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or upset if you’re interrupted while doing these repetitive actions, like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator?\n\nPatient: Not exactly anxious, I guess… more embarrassed if someone notices. Like at work I’ll start lining the receipts across the floor, and it’s like I’m trying to make them make sense, but then someone walks by and I realize it probably looks really weird.\n\nSame with the calculator—tapping it in nines. I don’t know why nine, except, like I said, baseball number, but that sounds stupid when I say it. And once I was just standing by the copier with my hands kind of held up, and I didn’t even really know how long until someone asked if I was okay. That part upset me after, more than during.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work and school, do these behaviors or your thoughts interfere with your daily life, like at home or with friends?\n\nPatient: I guess outside of that it’s… harder to say. Most of what I notice is work and school, because that’s where it shows up on paper, you know? Like the ledgers came back with real mistakes, not just little messy things, and I failed that course module, which still makes me feel sick when I think about it.\n\nAt home it’s more like I bring the stress back with me, but the big problem is I can’t reliably finish the stuff I used to just do. I’ve had to ask people to help me finish work that used to be routine, and that’s embarrassing. Like, I used to be the person who could handle those boring number tasks. Now I’m not sure I can trust myself with them.\n\nDoctor: Has this been affecting how you feel about yourself or how you interact with others, like feeling ashamed, avoiding situations, or having trouble with relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I still care about people. It’s not like I don’t. But I’ll see a text and think, “I should answer that,” and then it just sits there. Sometimes for days, which makes me look like I’m being rude.\n\nAnd after class, if people are going somewhere, I usually just skip it now. I used to like going to music shows and helping with art stuff—my friend did these poster designs once with, like, too many colors, but in a good way—and now when they talk about it I feel kind of… flat. Like I know I should be excited, or I remember being excited, but I’m not really there.\n\nSo yeah, I guess it makes me feel bad about myself. More like I’m disappearing from people than fighting with them.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or losing interest in things you used to enjoy, like going to music shows or helping with art?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. I don’t know if “down” is the exact word all the time, but I do feel sort of flat. Like my friends will talk about a show, or some art thing they’re doing, and I can remember that I used to be into that, like I’d have opinions about the poster or the lineup or whatever… but now I just don’t really feel the pull to go.\n\nAnd I still care about them, I do. It’s not like I’m mad at anyone. I’ll see a text and mean to answer it, then I don’t, and then it’s been two days and it feels awkward, so I put it off more. After class, if people are hanging out, I usually just skip it. It’s easier to go home, even though then I feel kind of bad about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any thoughts or ideas that keep coming up, even if you don’t want them, that bother you or make you feel uneasy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Like, thoughts that keep coming back… I mean, I think about the mistakes a lot, but that feels more like worrying or replaying it, not some specific idea I can’t get out of my head.\n\nSorry, I’m trying to answer it right. Sometimes I get stuck thinking about a text I didn’t answer, and then I’ll go clean something random or check my bag for receipts, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean. I wouldn’t say there’s one thought that keeps bothering me in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any thoughts or ideas that keep coming into your mind that you don’t want or that upset you, even if you try not to think about them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that exact way. I mean, I get thoughts about stuff I haven’t done, like messages sitting there, or whether I made something weird by not answering, but it’s more like I drift into it.\n\nIt’s not like one specific idea barges in and I’m fighting it off, if that makes sense. Sometimes I start thinking about it and then I’m suddenly remembering some totally unrelated thing, like where I put a receipt or whether I left my notebook in the car, and then I lose the thread.\n\nSo… I’m not sure I’d call it unwanted thoughts. More just my mind getting stuck on ordinary stuff and then wandering off.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless or losing pleasure in most things, not just the behaviors we’ve talked about, like work, school, or social activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it’s more than just the specific stuff. I don’t know if I’d say “hopeless” exactly, but most days I feel kind of flat. Like I still care about people, I’m not trying to ignore them, but I’ll let texts just sit there and then it gets harder to answer.\n\nAfter class, if people are hanging out, I usually just don’t go. And when friends talk about music shows or art projects, I remember I used to get excited about that stuff, like really into it, and now it’s more like… I hear them talking and I know I should want to go, but I don’t feel much of a pull. Sometimes I’ll just go home and then waste time looking at stuff I don’t even care about, which is weird because then the whole evening is gone.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that feel strange or upsetting, that you wish you could stop thinking about, even if you don’t act on them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like strange thoughts that I’m trying to shove away. I mean, I get uncomfortable thoughts sometimes, like replaying if I said something dumb, but that feels more like normal embarrassment that just drags on.\n\nSorry, I keep circling it. I guess no, I haven’t noticed a specific upsetting thought that keeps coming back like that. Sometimes my head jumps around a lot, but it’s more scattered than one thing I wish I could stop thinking about.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when a particular thought came into your mind that you really didn’t want to have, that bothered you, and you tried to push it away or get rid of it?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of one, but… no, not really. Like, I can think of times I didn’t want to deal with something, like an email from work or a text I let sit too long, and then I keep kind of bumping into it in my head.\n\nBut that’s not really a thought that pops in and I’m like, “get out of my head.” It’s more like I’ll remember it, then think about something else, then somehow I’m thinking about whether I paid a bill or where I left my keys. So… I don’t think I’ve had that kind of particular thought, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a thought come into your mind that you really didn’t want, that upset you, and that you tried hard not to think about—like something disturbing or inappropriate that kept coming back no matter how hard you tried to push it away?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I get thoughts I don’t like, but not in that way where it’s disturbing and keeps forcing itself back in.\n\nLike sometimes I’ll remember something awkward from class or work and then I’ll think about how I probably sounded weird, and then somehow I’m thinking about whether I forgot to submit something or if my room is a mess, and it kind of turns into this whole chain. But I’m not, like, fighting off one specific inappropriate thought over and over. At least I haven’t noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a thought come into your mind that felt completely wrong or disturbing, that you wished you could erase, even if you never acted on it?\n\nPatient: Not really… I’m trying to check if I’m missing something, because “completely wrong or disturbing” sounds pretty specific. I’ve had thoughts I didn’t like, like worrying I messed something up, or remembering something embarrassing, and then my brain kind of wanders off into other stuff.\n\nBut I can’t think of one that felt, like, horrible or wrong and I wanted to erase it. Nothing like that really comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a thought that felt intrusive, meaning it came into your mind even though you didn’t want it, and it upset you enough that you tried to stop it or do something else to make yourself feel better?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not like that. I mean, I’ll get uncomfortable thoughts sometimes, like if I remember I didn’t finish something at work, and then I start thinking about my desk or the spreadsheet I was using, and then I’m thinking about lunch or whether I left the lights on.\n\nBut it’s not like one thought breaks in and really upsets me and I have to do something to calm it down. I haven’t really noticed that happening.\n\nDoctor: When you’re doing these repetitive actions, like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator, do you feel like you have to do them a certain way to avoid something bad happening or to make things feel 'just right'?\n\nPatient: Not really to stop something bad from happening, I don’t think. No, I don’t think it’s really about anxiety or stopping something bad. It’s more like I get caught in it.

Like at work, I’ll start lining receipts across the floor, and then I’m just… doing that, even though it doesn’t make much sense. Or I tap the calculator in nines, and I don’t know why it has to be nine, it just sort of becomes the pattern. Nine is kind of a weird number anyway, like three threes, and then I start thinking about how calculators have those little rubber buttons—

But yeah, I don’t feel like something bad will happen if I stop. It’s more that I don’t realize how odd it looks until someone says something, like when I’m standing by the copier with my hands up and someone asks if I’m okay. Then I sort of snap back and feel embarrassed.

More just my mind getting stuck on ordinary stuff and then wandering off.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless or losing pleasure in most things, not just the behaviors we’ve talked about, like work, school, or social activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it’s more than just the specific stuff. Like I still care about people, I’m not trying to ignore them, but I’ll let texts just sit there and then it gets harder to answer.\n\nAfter class, if people are hanging out, I usually just don’t go. Not even calculating anything sometimes. Just tap-tap-tap until it feels… I don’t know, finished. So… that’s why I’m here, I think.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do these things, like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator, to prevent something bad from happening or because you get uncomfortable thoughts if you don’t?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s like… to stop something bad, exactly. I haven’t really thought of it that way. It’s more like I’m already doing it and then I notice I’m doing it.\n\nLike the calculator thing, it’s just there, and the buttons feel kind of organized, if that makes sense. My old math teacher used to say bookkeeping is supposed to be clean and balanced, but receipts are never clean, they’re all crumpled and coffee-stained and people hand them in late. Sorry—um, no, I don’t think I’m trying to prevent something. I just get caught in it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts, images, or urges that come into your mind that you don’t want or that bother you, like something bad happening or needing to do something in a certain way even if you don’t want to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I don’t think I get, like, a scary picture in my head and then I have to do something. It’s not like that exactly.\n\nSometimes I just notice the order of things, like if the papers are uneven or the numbers on the calculator—well, the nine thing is kind of funny because nine was my baseball number when I was a kid, but that probably doesn’t matter. I don’t know if I’d call it an urge. It’s more like my attention goes there and then I’m doing it before I really decide anything.\n\nDoctor: Do these behaviors ever bother you or make it hard for you to get things done at work or school?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s definitely getting in the way. I mean, I used to be pretty good with routine stuff, like ledgers and entering things—kind of boring, but I could do it.\n\nNow some of my ledgers have come back with serious errors, like not just a tiny typo. And at school I failed an important course module, which… I keep thinking about how the instructor had this blue pen he used for corrections, and I hated seeing it all over the page. Sorry. But yeah, I failed it.\n\nAt work I’ve had to ask other people to help me finish things because I can’t really count on myself to get through stuff that used to be normal for me. It’s embarrassing.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble paying attention or staying focused on tasks for most of your life, even before these behaviors with the receipts and calculator started?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I wasn’t like… thinking about attention back then, you know? In school I could get through things if I had to, mostly. Some classes were harder to sit through, but I think that’s normal.\n\nI used to doodle in the margins a lot—like little boxes and patterns, and one teacher said my notebook looked like graph paper that got confused. Sorry, that’s not really what you asked. I guess I haven’t noticed it being a “most of my life” kind of thing. It feels more like I’m just not getting through things now, but I don’t know if that’s because of focus exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or upset if you’re interrupted while doing these repetitive actions, like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator?\n\nPatient: Not exactly anxious, I guess… more embarrassed if someone notices. Like at work I’ll start lining the receipts across the floor, and it’s like I’m trying to make them make sense, but then someone walks by and I realize it probably looks really weird.\n\nSame with the calculator—tapping it in nines. I don’t know why nine, except, like I said, baseball number, but that sounds stupid when I say it. And once I was just standing by the copier with my hands kind of held up, and I didn’t even really know how long until someone asked if I was okay. That part upset me after, more than during.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work and school, do these behaviors or your thoughts interfere with your daily life, like at home or with friends?\n\nPatient: I guess outside of that it’s… harder to say. Most of what I notice is work and school, because that’s where it shows up on paper, you know? Like the ledgers came back with real mistakes, not just little messy things, and I failed that course module, which still makes me feel sick when I think about it.\n\nAt home it’s more like I bring the stress back with me, but the big problem is I can’t reliably finish the stuff I used to just do. I’ve had to ask people to help me finish work that used to be routine, and that’s embarrassing. Like, I used to be the person who could handle those boring number tasks. Now I’m not sure I can trust myself with them.\n\nDoctor: Has this been affecting how you feel about yourself or how you interact with others, like feeling ashamed, avoiding situations, or having trouble with relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I still care about people. It’s not like I don’t. But I’ll see a text and think, “I should answer that,” and then it just sits there. Sometimes for days, which makes me look like I’m being rude.\n\nAnd after class, if people are going somewhere, I usually just skip it now. I used to like going to music shows and helping with art stuff—my friend did these poster designs once with, like, too many colors, but in a good way—and now when they talk about it I feel kind of… flat. Like I know I should be excited, or I remember being excited, but I’m not really there.\n\nSo yeah, I guess it makes me feel bad about myself. More like I’m disappearing from people than fighting with them.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or losing interest in things you used to enjoy, like going to music shows or helping with art?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. I don’t know if “down” is the exact word all the time, but I do feel sort of flat. Like my friends will talk about a show, or some art thing they’re doing, and I can remember that I used to be into that, like I’d have opinions about the poster or the lineup or whatever… but now I just don’t really feel the pull to go.\n\nAnd I still care about them, I do. It’s not like I’m mad at anyone. I’ll see a text and mean to answer it, then I don’t, and then it’s been two days and it feels awkward, so I put it off more. After class, if people are hanging out, I usually just skip it. It’s easier to go home, even though then I feel kind of bad about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any thoughts or ideas that keep coming up, even if you don’t want them, that bother you or make you feel uneasy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Like, thoughts that keep coming back… I mean, I think about the mistakes a lot, but that feels more like worrying or replaying it, not some specific idea I can’t get out of my head.\n\nSorry, I’m trying to answer it right. Sometimes I get stuck thinking about a text I didn’t answer, and then I’ll go clean something random or check my bag for receipts, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean. I wouldn’t say there’s one thought that keeps bothering me in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any thoughts or ideas that keep coming into your mind that you don’t want or that upset you, even if you try not to think about them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that exact way. I mean, I get thoughts about stuff I haven’t done, like messages sitting there, or whether I made something weird by not answering, but it’s more like I drift into it.\n\nIt’s not like one specific idea barges in and I’m fighting it off, if that makes sense. Sometimes I start thinking about it and then I’m suddenly remembering some totally unrelated thing, like where I put a receipt or whether I left my notebook in the car, and then I lose the thread.\n\nSo… I’m not sure I’d call it unwanted thoughts. More just my mind getting stuck on ordinary stuff and then wandering off.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless or losing pleasure in most things, not just the behaviors we’ve talked about, like work, school, or social activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it’s more than just the specific stuff. I don’t know if I’d say “hopeless” exactly, but most days I feel kind of flat. Like I still care about people, I’m not trying to ignore them, but I’ll let texts just sit there and then it gets harder to answer.\n\nAfter class, if people are hanging out, I usually just don’t go. And when friends talk about music shows or art projects, I remember I used to get excited about that stuff, like really into it, and now it’s more like… I hear them talking and I know I should want to go, but I don’t feel much of a pull. Sometimes I’ll just go home and then waste time looking at stuff I don’t even care about, which is weird because then the whole evening is gone.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that feel strange or upsetting, that you wish you could stop thinking about, even if you don’t act on them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like strange thoughts that I’m trying to shove away. I mean, I get uncomfortable thoughts sometimes, like replaying if I said something dumb, but that feels more like normal embarrassment that just drags on.\n\nSorry, I keep circling it. I guess no, I haven’t noticed a specific upsetting thought that keeps coming back like that. Sometimes my head jumps around a lot, but it’s more scattered than one thing I wish I could stop thinking about.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when a particular thought came into your mind that you really didn’t want to have, that bothered you, and you tried to push it away or get rid of it?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of one, but… no, not really. Like, I can think of times I didn’t want to deal with something, like an email from work or a text I let sit too long, and then I keep kind of bumping into it in my head.\n\nBut that’s not really a thought that pops in and I’m like, “get out of my head.” It’s more like I’ll remember it, then think about something else, then somehow I’m thinking about whether I paid a bill or where I left my keys. So… I don’t think I’ve had that kind of particular thought, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a thought come into your mind that you really didn’t want, that upset you, and that you tried hard not to think about—like something disturbing or inappropriate that kept coming back no matter how hard you tried to push it away?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I get thoughts I don’t like, but not in that way where it’s disturbing and keeps forcing itself back in.\n\nLike sometimes I’ll remember something awkward from class or work and then I’ll think about how I probably sounded weird, and then somehow I’m thinking about whether I forgot to submit something or if my room is a mess, and it kind of turns into this whole chain. But I’m not, like, fighting off one specific inappropriate thought over and over. At least I haven’t noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a thought come into your mind that felt completely wrong or disturbing, that you wished you could erase, even if you never acted on it?\n\nPatient: Not really… I’m trying to check if I’m missing something, because “completely wrong or disturbing” sounds pretty specific. I’ve had thoughts I didn’t like, like worrying I messed something up, or remembering something embarrassing, and then my brain kind of wanders off into other stuff.\n\nBut I can’t think of one that felt, like, horrible or wrong and I wanted to erase it. Nothing like that really comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a thought that felt intrusive, meaning it came into your mind even though you didn’t want it, and it upset you enough that you tried to stop it or do something else to make yourself feel better?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not like that. I mean, I’ll get uncomfortable thoughts sometimes, like if I remember I didn’t finish something at work, and then I start thinking about my desk or the spreadsheet I was using, and then I’m thinking about lunch or whether I left the lights on.\n\nBut it’s not like one thought breaks in and really upsets me and I have to do something to calm it down. I haven’t really noticed that happening.\n\nDoctor: When you’re doing these repetitive actions, like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator, do you feel like you have to do them a certain way to avoid something bad happening or to make things feel 'just right'?\n\nPatient: Not really to stop something bad from happening, I don’t think. It’s more like… I just start doing it and then I’m in it.\n\nLike with the receipts, I’ll line them up across the floor at work, and I know that’s not really what I’m supposed to be doing, but somehow it feels like that’s the thing I’m doing. Or I’ll tap the calculator in sets of nine, and I don’t even know why nine. Nine is just the number my brain lands on, I guess.\n\nAnd the copier thing is weird too, because I’ll be standing there with my hands up, and then someone asks if I’m okay, and then I kind of realize it looks strange. But it’s not like I’m thinking, “If I don’t do this, something terrible will happen.” It’s more like I get stuck doing stuff that doesn’t make sense.\n\nDoctor: When you're doing these repetitive actions, like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator, do you feel a strong need to finish them in a certain way to feel less anxious or to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s really about anxiety or stopping something bad. It’s more like I get caught in it.\n\nLike at work, I’ll start lining receipts across the floor, and then I’m just… doing that, even though it doesn’t make much sense. Or I tap the calculator in nines, and I don’t know why it has to be nine, it just sort of becomes the pattern. Nine is kind of a weird number anyway, like three threes, and then I start thinking about how calculators have those little rubber buttons—\n\nBut yeah, I don’t feel like something bad will happen if I stop. It’s more that I don’t realize how odd it looks until someone says something, like when I’m standing by the copier with my hands up and someone asks if I’m okay. Then I sort of snap back and feel embarrassed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a thought come into your mind that felt intrusive — meaning it came up even though you didn’t want it — and it upset you enough that you tried to stop it or do something else to make yourself feel better?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so. I mean, thoughts pop in and out, sure, but not like… one that feels like it attacked me or something.\n\nSometimes I’ll be thinking about work stuff, like numbers not matching, and then I’m remembering the sound the adding machine makes, and then I’m thinking about how my old math teacher used to click his pen the whole class, which was really annoying. But I don’t think I’m trying to cancel a thought out or do some special thing so I feel better.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t noticed that in the way you’re describing."
